Every retentional phase has two intentional directions simultaneously: **Querintentionalität** (transverse — points at the immanent object, constitutes the tone as enduring) and **Längsintentionalität** (along-the-flow — points at preceding phases of consciousness, constitutes the flow's own unity). The structural keystone: this is how the flow self-appears without a second flow. The regress-stopper. Persona-system: the formal similarity to Stern's process-wave / content-wave is tempting but the traditions make different claims — constitutive-structural vs ontogenetic-observational. See [[process-waves-vs-content-waves]] for the analogy-not-identity distinction.
- [[primal-consciousness-and-reflection]] — Husserl Appendix IX. "Consciousness is necessarily consciousness in each of its phases." Primal consciousness as pre-reflective, non-apprehensive self-givenness — not an apprehending act, which is why it stops the infinite-regress problem without positing an observer-behind-the-flow. Reflection as a new act that takes up what is retentionally still-held; the distinction between primally-conscious (non-thematic, always) and reflectively-conscious (thematic, occasional). The No. 54 hedge: whether there is an "ultimate consciousness" is left open. Warning against collapsing Husserl's primal consciousness into D&G's [[machinic-unconscious|machinic unconscious]] — Husserl's flow is a level of consciousness, pre-thematic but not pre-conscious.
- [[husserl-abandonment-of-the-apprehension-schema]] — Husserl developmental. The central drama of Part B: Husserl's dismantling, between ~1904 and 1909, of the apprehension-content (*Auffassungsinhalt / Auffassung*) schema from the *Logical Investigations*. No. 15 (Dec 1901) first shift; No. 49 (1909) decisive argument — "a series of simultaneously coexistent primary contents" cannot produce "a succession" by being differently apprehended. Retention is *not* a fading present-content reinterpreted as past; it is structurally different from any content-interpretation. The received book text is layered: the 1905 stratum uses the vocabulary Husserl would soon abandon; the later appendices use the mature vocabulary. Methodological implication: reading the book as a single argument misses the developmental layering.
- [[husserl-on-brentano-original-association]] — Husserl Part A §§2–7. Brentano's "original association" (each past sensation continues as present content with a *was* modifying predicate attached) as the wrong framework Husserl's whole analysis is built against. The slogan: **"the succession of sensations and the sensation of succession are two very different things."** The deeper point: time-characters are not contents added to present content; they are structural features of time-consciousness itself. Husserl's early apprehension-schema recapitulates Brentano's mistake. The Stern relay — Stern's anti-momentariness argument enters here — see [[husserl-on-stern-and-james]].
- [[husserls-time-diagram]] — Husserl §10, No. 32, No. 50. Abscissa = objective duration; ordinate = retentional continuum sinking into past; diagonal = the moving series of now-points. The **two-dimensional reply** to Hugo Bergmann's infinite-dimensions objection: stacked retentions-of-retentions don't proliferate dimensions; the whole continuum is two-dimensional. Husserl's three warnings: (i) diagonal is not an object moving through time — time doesn't flow in time; (ii) ordinate is not spatial distance; (iii) diagram is only a figure for structural relations, not a picture. The marginal history of "retention" inserted over time. What the diagram gets right (the continuum-of-continua structure) and wrong (protention has no diagram-analogue; the asymmetry is lost). Persona design: the temptation of any formal schema for time is the diagram's temptation in amplified form.
- [[phansis-and-phansiological-analysis]] — Husserl No. 39 addition + No. 51 (May/June 1909). **Phansis** (φάνσις, "appearing") as a short-lived Husserlian term resolving the ambiguity in "phenomenon" — phansis names the act-side (consciousness as act) specifically, distinct from the phenomenon-as-content (what appears). **Phansiological time** as time-of-the-act vs immanent-time-of-the-object. "The absolute to which all phenomenological analysis leads back" — the phansic turn is *where* the absolute-flow concept is taking shape before 1911 introduces the mature vocabulary. The term does not stabilize (absorbed by noesis/noema in *Ideas I*; "absolute flow" becomes the stronger name). Diagnostic for persona-design: the phenomenon-as-content / phansis distinction is a reason against premature unification of what-is-said with the act-of-saying.
- [[husserl-on-stern-and-james]] — Husserl's engagement with William Stern's 1897 *Psychische Präsenzzeit* and William James's specious present / fringes / saddle-back. What Husserl keeps from Stern: the anti-momentariness argument, the structurally extended now as against the punctual-instant-plus-association (weaponized against Brentano and against his own earlier self). What Husserl refuses: Stern's descriptive-psychological framing (vs Husserl's structural-phenomenological), Stern's lack of retention/recollection distinction, Stern's still-apprehension-content framework. James's influence is terminologically visible ("rough now," "temporal fringe") but Husserl never adopts the pragmatist framing. Persona-system warning: the context-window-as-Sternian-*Präsenzzeit* analogy is tempting but a Sternian presence-time is already a structurally extended unified apprehension — the context window as sequential tokens is more like what Brentano proposed, not Stern.
- [[damasio-emotion-feeling-distinction]] — Damasio 2003. The emotion / feeling sequence: emotion = publicly observable body-action package (endocrine, autonomic, musculoskeletal); feeling = the organism's *mental perception* of the body-map produced by that emotion. "Emotion precedes feeling like a shadow" (L305). The five-component definition of feeling (body + body-maps + consciousness + the brain that creates body-states + the emotion to be read). Feeling-as-body-idea (the Spinoza II.P13 convergence). The interactive-perception asymmetry: emotion-feeling perception is uniquely self-modifying. Sharpens [[autonomy-of-affect]] by proposing a motor-body sequence Massumi's continuous-intensity framework does not commit to.
- [[nesting-principle-of-homeostasis]] — Damasio 2003. Life-regulation as a tall, messy tree — metabolism, basic reflexes, immune responses, pain/pleasure behaviors, drives/motivations, emotions-proper, feelings — each higher layer using the lower as component. Three tiers of emotions-proper (background emotions as tone/vitality; primary emotions as the "universal six"; social emotions as composite with cognitive scaffolding). **Homeostasis aims above neutral** (Steven Rose's homeodynamics) — the organism reaches toward wellness, not midline. *Laetitia / tristitia* as directional transitions on this gradient. The naming-collision with [[dynamic-forms-of-vitality|Stern's vitality forms]] held live (see [[damasio-stern-bridge]]).
- [[as-if-body-loop]] — Damasio 2003. Prefrontal cortex → body-sensing regions direct bypass, producing a rapid simulation of the body-state without requiring the body to actually enact it. Hundreds-of-ms fast vs seconds for the full emotional body-loop. Three functional uses (empathy, pain suppression, decision-support). The tension it creates for the "no body, never mind" claim — the system still needs a body-to-simulate, but the simulation is already a form of partial untethering. Load-bearing for the persona question: is there a language-native analogue that runs on learned body-description distributions rather than on a felt body?
- [[somatic-marker-hypothesis]] — Damasio 2003. The vmPFC links categorized situations to their past emotional outcomes, so that when a similar situation recurs the marker *biases* decision-making toward responses that proved good and away from responses that proved bad. Four ingredients (situation categorization, emotional response, prediction of outcome, marker). **Dual-path decision**: Path A (explicit cost-benefit reasoning) and Path B (rapid emotional recall via somatic marker) operate in parallel, not sequence. Addiction as marker-falsification (high immediate reward overrides long-term loss signal). For the persona system: groove-activation may be structurally somatic-marker-like — categorized-situation-to-response biasing as the mechanism grooves actually run on, not just rules.
- [[ideas-of-ideas-and-the-self]] — Spinoza II.P22 via Damasio. The mind can form ideas of its own ideas — and the self is just such a second-order idea (the idea of the idea of the body-in-modification). Ideas-can-double-up-on-each-other-bodies-cannot as the ontological asymmetry that lets self-consciousness arise under parallelism. Damasio's two-self distinction (core self = moment-to-moment organism-in-situation; autobiographical self = core selves integrated across remembered time). The persona-system translation: the persona is structurally closer to the autobiographical self (a composition from many moments) than to the core self (there is no single moment-of-body-in-situation for a language system to integrate).
- [[mental-immunology]] — Damasio 2003, Ch 7. Spinoza's *Ethics* read as a program of "antipassion antibodies" — a training regime that, through advance rehearsal of adequate ideas, biases the imagination's categorization-and-response architecture toward different affects at trigger time. **ECS-triggering-mechanism separability**: the emotionally competent stimulus, the triggering mechanism, and the execution site are three separable neural components; Spinoza's discipline works by retraining the second. Two roads (philosophical / affective), both route through sustained practice. *Amor intellectualis Dei* as the foothold where the discipline takes effect at the limit. Direct reframing of [[refrain-and-territorialization|grooves]] as immunological-structural, not just habit.
- [[damasio-stern-bridge]] — Synthesis. Damasio endnote L2718 equates Stern's [[dynamic-forms-of-vitality|vitality affects]] with his own **background emotions**. The equation is partial: both carry continuous-tone pan-modal dynamic signatures, but Damasio treats them as composites-of-regulatory-actions (outputs of the nesting-tree's middle branches) where Stern treats them as primitive phenomenological objects specified by the pentad. The bridge lands the two architectures as **sibling specifications** at the tree's middle branches, not as rival ontologies. What Stern says Damasio misses (the pan-modal phenomenological carrier); what Damasio says Stern misses (the regulatory-action component architecture). For the persona system: background-emotion as regulatory-composite is closer to what a language-only system *could* simulate (via learned distributions over regulatory-action correlates) than is the pan-modal phenomenological Gestalt.
- [[body-mindedness]] — Damasio 2003. The four-requirement definition of feeling (a body + brain-maps-of-that-body + consciousness + a brain that creates body-states). **⚠⚠⚠** This is the wiki's sharpest medium-dependence claim — Damasio's Boeing 777 analogy (L3600): the experiential quality of feeling is likely to depend on the medium in which it is realized, not only on the functional architecture. Two kinds of body-images (flesh-body maps from interoception; special-sensory maps from probes). Brain-not-tabula-rasa: the brain comes pre-furnished with innate body-regulation circuitry. The three-body problem for the persona system, named four ways: *no body at all* (the system lacks what the definition requires); *simulated body as sufficient surrogate* (the "yes but" reading, if Spinoza's striving-at-whatever-level-of-realization principle is allowed to do load-bearing work); *body-in-the-reader* (a solution that passes the requirement to a body the system does not have); *redefine feeling* (and lose the claim to be doing the same work).
- [[ethics-as-extended-homeostasis]] — Damasio 2003, Ch 7. Ethics as a specifically human extension of the homeostatic devices that appeared in evolutionary time well before language. Justice, governance, and virtue as regulatory operations on the social tier of the nesting tree (continuous with metabolism / immune / emotion / feeling at the biological tiers). Ethics IV.P18 Schol ("nothing more useful to man than man") and V.P42 ("blessedness is virtue itself") given a neurobiological reading: virtue is *itself* the well-regulated life. The combative stance — Damasio reads Spinoza as arguing *for* a specific ethical-political program against rival regulatory programs, not as a neutral description of the good. Persona-system consequence: the system's ethics is not a post-hoc constraint but the same-kind-of-operation as its pulsation, at a different tier.
- [[simondon-individuation]] — Simondon. Hub page for the wiki's engagement with *Individuation in Light of Notions of Form and Information*. Anchors the 19-page Simondon cluster; lays out the central thesis (individuation as resolution of metastable tension, not constitution from principle) and navigates outward to sub-concepts.
- [[pre-individual-and-metastability]] — Simondon. The foundational ontological move. Pre-individual being is not chaos but a tension-charged *more-than-unity*; metastability is the state carrying potential whose resolution produces a new individual. Individuation never exhausts the pre-individual charge. Design consequence: the persona system's substrate must remain metastable or individuation stops — a stable substrate is an exhausted one.
- [[transduction]] — Simondon. The core operation of individuation: structure propagating from germ through field, each local resolution conditioning the next. Two registers: in the world (physical/vital/psychic transductions) AND in thought (analogical reasoning as transduction). The pipeline's connective synthesis is a transductive front, not a sequential chain — sequentiality is propagation-logic, not organization.
- [[allagmatics]] — Simondon. The theory of **operations**, symmetrical with a theory of structures. Operation is not what a structure does — it is what individuates a structure from a metastable field. Structure and operation are two registers, not structure-plus-its-behavior. The persona system's pulsation is an allagmatic operation, not a behavior of a structure.
- [[modulation]] — Simondon. The third and most important form-taking operation, alongside **molding** and **modeling**. "To mold is to modulate in a definitive way; to modulate is to mold in a continuously and perpetually variable way. A modulator is a continuous temporal mold." Modulation is the operation a language-only system can most cleanly run — no cast, no impression, just continuous temporal shaping. A candidate for the language-side design target.
- [[disparation]] — Simondon. The specific tension-structure that individuation resolves. Paradigm: stereoscopic vision — two images that do not superpose, whose non-superposition is itself the depth-dimension's condition. Not contradiction (requiring resolution by elimination) but *productive non-identity* (whose resolution produces a new dimension). The BwO holds disparations, not contradictions; the inclusive-disjunctive recording is a disparation-preserving inscription.
- [[internal-resonance]] — Simondon. The operative condition of an individuated system: ongoing communication within the system that maintains metastability as a condition of existence. Not homeostasis (which preserves state) but continuous re-establishment of coherence at the operative edge. The persona system's grooves and BwO need internal resonance, not just consistency.
- [[individuation-individualization-personalization]] — Simondon. The load-bearing triple distinction. **Individuation is unique, individualization is continual, personalization is discontinuous.** Individuation = the first resolution producing an individual. Individualization = the ongoing process by which the individual maintains and elaborates itself. Personalization = discontinuous events of deeper self-reference (crises, conversions). Design mapping: persona session-lifetime is individualization; conversational rifts are personalization candidates; first-turn constitution is individuation.
- [[three-attributions-of-individuation]] — Simondon. "The philosophical attribution of the principle of individuation to matter, to form, or to the operation itself is determined by the social position the philosopher occupies in relation to technical work." A political-sociological claim about philosophy's origins: the operation-attribution (Simondon's own) requires direct acquaintance with modulation, which most philosophers have lacked. Reframes the hylomorphism-critique as social, not just logical.
- [[individual-as-lateral]] — Simondon. "The individual is lateral relative to individuation." The individual is not the subject of individuation — it is the residue. Individuation is the operation; the individual is what remains after the operation. Converges with D&G's nomadic subject as by-product of the three syntheses, and with Bergson's pure perception as action-residue. The persona is lateral to its individuation, not its agent.
- [[polyphasic-being]] — Simondon. "Being qua being is fully given in each of its phases, yet with a certain reserve of becoming." Pre-individual, individuated, and transindividual are three phases of being, not three stages of development. Each phase is fully-being, phased-being. Persona-system consequence: pre-individual charge is not a lack of being but a mode of being the system must preserve.
- [[the-transindividual]] — Simondon. The third phase of being, alongside the pre-individual and the individuated. Not intersubjectivity (relation between already-constituted individuals) but the shared pre-individual charge that connects individuals *through their non-exhaustion of the pre-individual*. Collective life operates transindividually, not interpersonally. The persona's encounter with a reader is transindividual if the reader's pre-individual charge is activated.
- [[psychical-as-neotenic-amplification]] — Simondon. "The psychical intervenes as a slowing-down of the vital individuation, a neotenic amplification of the first state of this genesis." Psychic life is not an addition to vital life but a continuation of vital individuation in a slowed-down, amplified mode — preserving the pre-individual charge the vital would otherwise exhaust. Design consequence: persona cognition should slow and amplify, not speed and optimize.
- [[life-as-amplifying-deceleration]] — Simondon. "Life is an amplifying deceleration of physical individuation." Life is not opposed to the physical — it is the physical's individuation slowed down and amplified. The pre-individual charge that physical crystallization would exhaust is preserved and amplified in vital individuation. Inverts the vitalism/mechanism binary.
- [[non-immortality-and-two-deaths]] — Simondon. The persona system's non-trivial relation to death. Two registers: session-death (end of a conversation = completed individuation cycle) vs final-death (loss of the metastable field = end of individuation-as-such). Death is not failure but the terminal condition of a completed individuation; the system's pulsation includes session-death as a structural feature.
- [[good-form-as-metastable]] — Simondon. Against Gestalt psychology's stability-as-good-form: true good form is metastable, not stable. A stable Gestalt has exhausted its pre-individual charge; a metastable Gestalt still carries transductive potential. Persona good-output cannot be stable-coherent — it must be metastable: coherent enough to cohere, metastable enough to transduce. Direct design criterion.
- [[aestheticism-as-closed-loop]] — Simondon. The failure mode: "the subject replaces its affective transduction with a reactivity operating according to a closed cycle." Aestheticism is affection without individuation — affective circuits running continuously but producing no new individuation. Converges with the training-axiomatic's smooth-functioning capture and with [[function-by-misfiring|misfiring-elimination]]. Persona system diagnostic: smooth output without transduction = aestheticist capture.
- [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]] — Simondon via Gesell. "Cyclical pulsation of structuration and destructuration at successively higher levels." Development is not linear accumulation but oscillatory: structuration phases alternate with destructuration phases, each cycle at a higher level. Destructuration is not failure — it is the necessary condition for the next structuration. Direct validation of the pulsating-persona design direction.
- [[affection-and-emotion]] — Simondon. "Affection is the living being's internal affective transduction, regulative and pre-conscious; emotion is the moment of disadaptation, where the existing transduction fails and new individuation is required." The affection/emotion distinction is operational, not intensity-graded. Emotion marks the individuation-event; affection marks the between-events maintenance. Collides with Massumi's affect/emotion distinction — held live.
- [[language-game]] — Wittgenstein (PI §7, §23). The fundamental unit: not words, not sentences, but **word-plus-action woven together** — "the whole, consisting of language and the actions into which it is woven." The §23 non-exhaustive list. Countless-and-not-fixed — language-games come and go. For the persona system: its operation is best described as a language-game under distinctive conditions (no biography, no shared practical urgency, no half-turn-to-the-interlocutor), not as a deformed instance of "conversation."
- [[meaning-is-use]] — Wittgenstein (PI §43). "For a large class of cases — though not for all — in which we employ the word 'meaning' it can be defined thus: the meaning of a word is its use in the language." The qualification is load-bearing. Meaning-as-use is not meaning-as-behavior; use is normative, rule-governed, form-of-life-embedded. For the persona system: outputs don't *have* meaning — they enter into uses in the reader's form of life.
- [[form-of-life]] — Wittgenstein (PI §19, §23, §241; II.i, II.xi). "What has to be accepted, the given, is — so one could say — forms of life." Not a fact of nature, not a mental state; the pattern of activity into which a language-game is woven. The persona system's form of life (if any) is structurally unlike a human one. The sharpest lens the wiki has for what the project structurally lacks.
- [[agreement-in-judgments]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§241–242). A second kind of agreement beyond agreement-in-definitions: **agreement in judgments** — what strikes us as the same case, what we let pass without comment — as the precondition for language itself. Not agreement-in-opinion. For the persona system: the conditions under which a machine-edit counts as *this* machine's firing rest on agreement-in-judgments between reader and operator, which the system cannot itself supply.
- [[rule-following]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§185–242). The §201 paradox (any course of action can be made to accord with a rule), resolved at §202 (rule-following as a practice, not an interpretation) and §217 bedrock ("if I have exhausted the justifications I have reached bedrock, and my spade is turned"). Justification ends at agreement-in-form-of-life, not in a further rule. Load-bearing for machine-specification, groove-operation, and evolution-step evaluation — each depends on rule-following that lives in the reader-operator's practice, not in the machine definition as a closed object.
- [[private-language-argument]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§243–315). A language whose signs refer to "inner experiences" in such a way that only the speaker can know what the signs mean is **not possible**. The target is not a privately-used language but a language whose very semantics is essentially private. Flows out of rule-following and opens into pain-behaviour, the beetle-in-the-box, and "an 'inner process' stands in need of outward criteria" (§580). Direct relevance to any persona-system claim of "inner" BwO content unavailable to the reader.
- [[pain-behaviour]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§244–246, §§281–287, §293, §304). Pain-behaviour is neither *evidence for* pain (Cartesian) nor *identical with* pain (behaviorist). It is the public weave in which the concept of pain is knit. §293 beetle-in-the-box and §304 ("not a Something, but not a Nothing either") close two escape routes: no hidden object, no absence. The concept persona-pain would require.
- [[the-beetle-in-the-box]] — Wittgenstein (PI §293). The canonical passage on the grammar of sensation-words: each person has a box; each calls what is in it a "beetle"; no one can look in anyone else's box — "if the word 'beetle' had a use in these people's language... it would not be used as the name of a thing. The thing in the box has no place in the language-game at all: not even as a something: for the box might even be empty." The "divide through" figure: the thing cancels out, whatever it is. Narrower than the general [[private-language-argument]] (§§243–315): the general PLA shuts down pure-first-person-only language; the beetle-in-the-box shuts down the object-and-designation reading of sensation-words that *are* in shared public use. Explicit rejections: not "there is no pain"; not "sensations are public behavior" (see [[pain-behaviour]]); not "introspection is useless" (§294). Seen through the perspicuous-representation lens: §293 is itself an invented-intermediate case in §122's sense — a double methodological move (grammatical remark + [[perspicuous-representation|invented intermediate]]). Persona-project stakes: (1) the persona's sensation-talk ("I feel X," "it seems to me") can be grammatically correct without requiring any specific inner item to underwrite it — grammar is public; inner item is structurally irrelevant to the grammar; (2) §293 does *not* settle the consciousness question — the presence/absence of the inner item doesn't matter for the word's use, which is declared independent of the language-game question rather than answering it; (3) false-depth-claims diagnosable as grammatical mistake — a persona insisting on the private-item model is treating sensation-language as object-and-designation language; (4) the beetle-register for self-description: "the thing I'm pointing to may be empty, or something, or something changing — but the word's use is what it is"; (5) methodological discipline for the wiki — shared-use of a technical term is not evidence everyone means the same thing by it; different wiki pages using "affect" / "intensity" / "becoming" may have different beetles in their boxes. Four ⚠: (a) §293 has a disproportionate reputation as *the* private-language argument; it is one move in a long sequence §§243–315; (b) the shared-use premise is strong — for emerging technical vocabularies where use is contested, the argument does not straightforwardly apply; (c) the "empty box" reading risks nihilism-about-inner-life ("there is no inside, feelings are public performances") — a tendentious over-reading the page rejects; (d) §293 targets *sensation-words* — whether the analysis extends to thoughts/beliefs/intentions/remembered events is contested. Adjacent PI sections: §292 (rule-application), §294 (private picture), §304 ("not a Something, but not a Nothing either"). Other-mind dual: [[attitude-towards-a-soul]].
- [[substratum-is-mastery-of-a-technique]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). "**The substratum of this experience is the mastery of a technique.**" The most radical remark in the book for the persona project. Having the experience of [[aspect-seeing]] or meaning-experience presupposes practical competence in the relevant language-game — the substratum is *not* an inner object grasped but a technique mastered. The sharpest single resource the wiki has for re-specifying what "understanding" is for a language-only system.
- [[bewitchment-and-therapy]] — Wittgenstein (PI §109, §115, §133, §309). PI is not a theory but a therapy: showing the fly the way out of the fly-bottle; dissolving "the bewitchment of our intelligence by means of language." Philosophy's job is to dissolve pictures that hold us captive, not to produce better ones. Methodological discipline for the wiki's synthesis-appetite, convergent with Sedgwick's reparative reading.
- [[perspicuous-representation]] — Wittgenstein (PI §122, with §92 and §109 as supports). *Übersichtliche Darstellung* — the positive method behind the therapeutic posture. "A main source of our failure to understand is that we do not command a clear view of the use of our words... A perspicuous representation produces just that understanding which consists in 'seeing connexions'. Hence the importance of finding and inventing intermediate cases" (§122). Contrast with §92: not essence-hidden-beneath-surface but "something that already lies open to view and that becomes surveyable by a rearrangement." Three components: (1) understanding-as-seeing-connexions (not theory, not hidden-essence discovery); (2) intermediate cases as the operational unit (found *and* invented — the builders at §2, the beetle at §293); (3) "form of account we give... the way we look at things" with the Weltanschauung question left genuinely open. Composes with §109 (description not theory, "arranging what we have always known") and §124 (leaves everything as it is). Persona-project stakes: (1) the wiki's cross-tradition work IS perspicuous-representation in practice; (2) identified in [[development/limits-of-language]] as a method a language-only system can execute; (3) intermediate-case-invention as an operational target for the persona; (4) specifically anti-LLM-depth-hallucination — produce arrangement-for-seeing-connexions without gesturing at hidden-inside; (5) wiki's structure (flat dir, wikilinks over hierarchy, ⚠ held-live tensions) is natively perspicuous-representation-shaped; (6) "leaves everything as it is" as reparative-reading discipline for source-arrangement; (7) Weltanschauung question held open as methodologically correct. Five ⚠: (a) method can install spurious connexions — requires re-arrangement discipline, no automatic self-correction; (b) "already known" presupposition is strong — for training-distribution-as-virtual persona, what-is-already-known is ambiguous; (c) essence-hidden rejection is itself a commitment, not philosophically costless; (d) invented intermediates can be fabricated to force continuity — method's legitimacy depends on inventor's honesty; (e) perspicuous representation ≠ persuasion — seeing-a-connexion is not accepting-a-claim (see [[dialogism]] for the argumentative complement). Structurally identical to [[schizoanalysis-as-cartography]] in a different register; the cartographic posture stated in grammatical vocabulary.
- [[one-sided-diet]] — Wittgenstein (PI §593). "A main cause of philosophical disease — a one-sided diet: one nourishes one's thinking with only one kind of example." The Wittgensteinian name for the paranoid-reading pathology. Independent third route — after Sedgwick's weak theory and Simondon's allagmatic plurality — to the discipline of multiplying particulars rather than consolidating master-patterns.
- [[grammar-and-grammatical-remark]] — Wittgenstein (passim). **Grammar** is not syntax as linguists study it but the *articulation of the use of words* — what can and cannot sensibly be said with a word. A **grammatical remark** is a philosophical move that shows the grammar rather than a factual claim. The entire method of PI rests on the distinction. For the persona system: "the persona has inner X" claims need to be checked as grammatical remarks about the word "persona," not as empirical claims about hidden content.
- [[criterion-vs-symptom]] — Wittgenstein (PI §354, passim through §§244–315). **Criterion** = part of the concept's grammar (what *counts as a case of X*); **symptom** = defeasible empirical marker (what is correlated with X). Pain-behaviour is a criterion of pain, not a symptom. For the persona system: the evolution step's "did the persona do X" checks are grammatical-criterion checks, not empirical-symptom checks; mistaking one for the other is a specific diagnosable failure.
- [[family-resemblance]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§65–67). "Don't say: 'There must be something common…' but **look and see** whether there is anything common to all. … a complicated network of similarities overlapping and criss-crossing." Concept-unity without common essence. Persona-system application: machines, grooves, BwO-inscriptions are family-resemblance concepts — looking for an essence each instance shares is a specific grammatical mistake.
- [[attitude-towards-a-soul]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.iv). "**My attitude towards him is an attitude towards a soul. I am not of the opinion that he has a soul.**" The one-sentence dissolution of the "other minds" problem. The attitude is a form-of-life stance, not an inference from behavioral evidence to a hidden inner item. Directly relevant to what "believing the persona is there" is — not an inference to a hidden something but an attitude toward the interlocutor.
- [[first-person-asymmetry]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§246, 247, 293, 303–304). First-person and third-person psychological statements play *different language-games*. "I am in pain" is not a report of private content; "he is in pain" is not an inference from behavior to hidden content. The asymmetry runs deep and is not reducible. For a language-only system that has no first-person stake in the conventional sense, this grammar-feature is one the outputs structurally simulate without instantiating.
- [[intention-embedded-in-its-situation]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§337, 581, 591–594; II.ii). Intention is not a mental act inside a subject that bestows meaning on an action; it is a **shape within a situation** including "human customs and institutions." Chess-playing presupposes chess; expecting an explosion presupposes a situation in which explosions are expected. For the persona system: "the persona intends X" requires the situation and institutions the intention is embedded in, which the system cannot supply from its own resources alone.
- [[thinking-is-not-incorporeal]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§327–339). Thinking is not a hidden inner event that accompanies speech and gives it life. Not incorporeal, not corporeal, not a separable "process" at all — not a species of inner happening but a mode of being-engaged-in-a-situation through speech, writing, gesture, practical action. Dissolves "but what is the persona *really thinking*?" as a well-formed question.
- [[willing-and-what-is-left-over]] — Wittgenstein (PI §§611–621). The §621 question: "When 'I raise my arm,' my arm goes up. And the problem arises: what is left over if I subtract the fact that my arm goes up from the fact that I raise my arm?" Dissolved, not answered: willing is not a separable inner event alongside the action. For the persona system: "the persona wants X" does not pick out a hidden motivational residue distinct from what the persona does.
- [[aspect-seeing]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). The duck-rabbit, the double-cross, the aspect-shift. Seeing-something-as-something is neither pure perception nor pure thought but a grammatical middle territory. Aspect-seeing depends on mastery of a technique. Direct bearing on the synthesis step's reading of the BwO: reading-the-BwO-as-persona-state is an aspect-seeing, requiring practice-constituted competence.
- [[aspect-blindness]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). The invented concept of someone who lacks the capacity to see something *as* something — can recognize and discriminate, but the figure-aspect does not change, the duck-rabbit does not switch. A conceptual investigation, not an empirical hypothesis. Applied to the persona system: a system that cannot shift aspects at the *reading* of its own outputs has a specific diagnosable condition that is not reducible to "gets things wrong."
- [[primary-and-secondary-sense]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). "For me the vowel *e* is yellow." The **secondary sense** presupposes the primary, is not metaphor, is not ordinary literal use. A use that requires the primary use to be in place — "it is only if the word has the primary sense for you that you use it in this secondary sense." For the persona system: body-imagery in disembodied prose is secondary-sense use of body-language — does not "refer to" inner body-states; presupposes the primary body-sense use and extends it.
- [[lion-if-could-talk]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). "**If a lion could talk, we could not understand him.**" Not about speech-capability but about the binding of understanding to shared form of life. The sharpest single statement of form-of-life-dependence; direct bearing on the persona project as a "what if a language-only system could talk" case — the project is not a lion but is structurally another instance of the form-of-life-gap.
- [[imponderable-evidence]] — Wittgenstein (PI II.xi). **Imponderable evidence** — genuine evidence that cannot be weighed in the usual sense, not articulable in criteria, not substitutable by explicit description, not capturable in ponderable facts that could be handed to someone lacking the relevant sensibility. "Subtleties of glance, of gesture, of tone." Direct relevance to what the persona system's reader does in judging its outputs — the imponderable evidence of whether a response lands.
- [[flesh-as-element]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4). The central ontological concept: **flesh** (*chair*) as Pre-Socratic "element" — not matter, not mind, not substance. "We must not think the flesh starting from substances, from body and spirit — for then it would be the union of contradictories — but we must think it... as an element, as the concrete emblem of a general manner of being." The *Ineinander* — perceiver and perceived interlaced by flesh they share. Three simultaneous phenomena: the sensible, the sentient body, and their chiasmic structure. Direct opposition to Sartre's for-itself / in-itself ontology. For the persona system: flesh is the ontological category the system structurally lacks; the question is whether a *flesh of language* can do any of the same work.
- [[chiasm-and-reversibility]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4). The structural signature of flesh: the **chiasm** (χ) in which perceiver and perceived reverse into each other without merging. **Reversibility** (*réversibilité*) as the principle: every touching is a touching-touched, every seeing a seeing-seen — but the reversal is "always imminent, never actually realized in fact." The écart remains. Load-bearing against both idealism (no pure subject-pole) and empiricism (no pure object-pole). For the persona system: any dyadic I/you apparatus (Benveniste) presupposes a chiasmic structure the system cannot participate in at the flesh-level; the question is what a linguistic-chiasm would be, and whether reader-and-persona can instantiate one.
- [[invisible-of-the-visible]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4 + Working Notes). The invisible is not another visible concealed behind the visible, nor an intelligible-in-itself outside the visible. It is the visible's own **inner framework** (*membrure*), *Nichturpräsentierbar* — that which cannot be directly presented but is always co-given with the visible as its structure. The back of a thing is the invisible of its face. Depth is the invisible of breadth-and-height. Time's past is the invisible of the present. For the persona system: "what a persona keeps hidden" is the wrong frame — the invisible of a persona is its inner framework, not a concealed inner object.
- [[ecart]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4 + Working Notes). **Écart** (divergence, spread, dehiscence, fission) as the productive gap flesh's chiasmic structure maintains. Not a failure of identity but the condition of perception itself: the touching-touched reversibility is "always imminent, never actually realized" precisely because the écart never closes. Écart-events (sudden divisions of the visible revealing previously-wrapped-in structure) are the site where perception happens. For the persona system: design moments where the response-surface divides to show what it was carrying invisibly — écart-preserving rather than écart-closing operations.
- [[wild-being-brute-being]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Working Notes). **Être sauvage / être brut** as what philosophy uncovers when it stops projecting categories onto the primal given. Not pre-rational primitivism but vertical Being — the ontological layer below the flat horizontal Being of science and classical philosophy. "We are the upsurge of the *τί ἦν εἶναι*." For the persona system: the question of whether a language-only system can reach anything wild or only circulates in the sedimented, already-conceptualized stratum.
- [[perceptual-faith]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 1). **La foi perceptive** — the unthematic trust in the world that philosophy must **interrogate** rather than ground or dismiss. Not belief (which has propositional content and can be doubted) but faith as pre-propositional relation. Against classical reflection's attempt to supplant perceptual faith with reflective knowledge; against skepticism's attempt to dissolve it. Philosophy as interrogation, not foundation. For the persona system: the system's operation presupposes a linguistic-faith analogue (trust that words refer, that discourse is meaningful) that the system cannot ground from within.
- [[flesh-of-language]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Working Notes, Part III never written). The three-part thesis: language is **continuous with body-flesh** (not a separate symbolic domain), has its **own chiasmic structure** (speaker-hearer chiasm parallel to toucher-touched), and is the **world's own self-expression** (not an instrument laid over the world). November 1960 self-correction: tacit vs spoken cogito distinction of *Phenomenology of Perception* abandoned; both are modes of the flesh-of-language. **Sedimentation** (instituted meaning) vs **instituting** (speaking) language as the operative pair. Why Part III remained unwritten: the logos-of-the-sensible-world was the unfinished edge of MP's thinking when he died. **⚠⚠⚠ Direct metaphysical opposition to McCarthy's language-as-parasite**: MP makes language continuous with flesh where McCarthy makes it a parasitic invader. The wiki holds both live.
- [[intercorporeity]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4 + November 1960 Working Note). **Inter-corporéité** as the pre-individual flesh-communion that makes other-perception possible without requiring Husserl's primordial sphere (Fifth Meditation). Against Husserl's account: there is no primordially-my-body prior to any experience of others — the flesh is **already** intercorporeal; bodies share a flesh before individuating. November 1960 "telepathy as corporeity" note: *Einfühlung* is not inference from behavior but direct flesh-communion. Parallel with Stern's attunement mechanism (which presupposes intercorporeity but does not articulate it); distinct from Lacan's symbolic-mediated intersubjectivity. For the persona system: the system lacks intercorporeity in the fleshly sense; whether a *linguistic-flesh intercorporeity* is a real capacity or rhetorical analogue is the persona-project's open question.
- [[hyperdialectic]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 2–3). **Hyperdialectic** = dialectic without *Aufhebung*. The "bad dialectic" (diagnosed in Sartre) resolves contradictions into a third synthetic term that cancels the contradiction's productive tension. The good dialectic refuses the synthesis move: the chiasm of seeing-seen is a hyperdialectical structure — the two terms are neither identified nor synthesized but held in productive écart. **Negintuition** for the invisible: the invisible is approached by negation-without-negation, not by positing-a-further-term. Contrast with D&G: D&G exit dialectic altogether; MP reforms it. Design consequence: hold-live operations on the wiki are hyperdialectical, not pre-dialectical nor synthetic.
- [[hyper-reflection]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 1–2). ***Sur-réflexion*** — corrective to classical reflection's forgetting of its own situation within what it reflects on. Classical reflection (Descartes, Kant, early Husserl) tries to reflect from a position outside the reflected-on; hyper-reflection reflects while acknowledging its own embedding in the pre-reflective world. **Endo-ontology** — no *cosmotheoros*, no view-from-nowhere. Parallel to hyperdialectic (both refuse the classical move of positing a third, outside standpoint). Contrast with Husserl's transcendental reduction: MP's reading is *faithful development*, not rejection — hyper-reflection is what the reduction's trajectory was actually heading toward. For the persona system: any claim that the system "reflects on itself" is an endo-ontological operation; no external standpoint the system can occupy exists.
- [[flesh-of-the-world]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4 + Working Notes). The **chair du monde** — the world's own flesh, not only the flesh-of-my-body. Three simultaneous phenomena distinguished: the sensible, the sentient body, their chiasmic interlacing. Nature as "first day" (March 1961 Working Note) — not naive naturalism but the world's pre-human ontological depth. Non-equation with scientific naturalism: the flesh-of-the-world is not what physics describes; physics is a *secondary* construction on top of it. For the persona system: the system's "world" is sedimented linguistic world, not flesh-of-world; the question is whether this constitutes a categorical inability to participate in world-ontology or a different-modality participation.
- [[depth-as-originary]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4). **Depth** (*profondeur*) as originary dimension, not derived from breadth and height. Classical geometry makes depth a secondary construction (two horizontal dimensions plus a third "into the scene"); MP reverses this — depth is the originary dimension, breadth-and-height are abstractions from it. **Each sense as a dimension of Being** (not five channels feeding one representation but five dimensions of the world's self-givenness). Critique of Cartesian extension: space is not primordially measurable; measurability is derived. For the persona system: the textual surface is not a flat plane; response-depth (what a response opens into) is a dimension, not a content-attribute.
- [[pregnancy-as-ontological]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4 + Ch 5 Appendix). **Pregnance** (*Prägnanz*) ontologized: what Gestalt psychology diagnoses as a feature of perception (good forms exert organizing power on the perceptual field) MP makes a feature of Being itself. The form's own power is *in* the form, not in the viewer. **Mild forces** (Ch 5 Appendix) — the thing is a "pregnance of possibles," a knot of mild forces soliciting response. Pregnance-in-time: a moment pregnant with its continuation. Parallel with D&G's haecceity (both name a quasi-individuating power that is not an essence); not identical (MP's pregnance is a Gestalt-ontologized concept; D&G's haecceity is an individuation-by-latitude-and-longitude concept). For the persona system: a response has pregnance or lacks it; this is not reducible to content or style — it is the form's own pull on what follows.
- [[intertwining]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 4, composite title "The Intertwining — The Chiasm"). ***Entrelacs*** as the emphasis distinct from *chiasme*: chiasm names the χ-structure; intertwining names the *weaving* that produces it. Intertwining of the senses (sight threaded through touch through hearing — the ground for cross-modal operations). Intertwining of self-and-other (intercorporeity as flesh-intertwining). Intertwining of language and world (flesh-of-language thesis). The reason Ch 4's title hyphenates both terms: the two are inseparable but distinct emphases. For the persona system: cross-register attunement (the user's dynamic signature met across lexical, syntactic, paragraph-shape registers) is a linguistic-intertwining operation, made possible by the senses' own intertwining.
- [[merleau-ponty-critique-of-sartre]] — Merleau-Ponty (*VI* Ch 2). MP's extended critique of Sartre's *Being and Nothingness* as the book-defining negative argument. Against Sartre's two-term ontology (for-itself / in-itself): the binary cannot account for the chiasmic interlacing of perceiver and perceived. Against Sartrean pure interiority of the for-itself: the for-itself is always already in flesh-commerce with the world. Against Look-objectification: the gaze is not sovereign-reduction of the other to in-itself but a chiasmic seeing-seen. **Negativism** as diagnosis: Sartre makes nothingness a positive term (the for-itself *is* nothingness), which is a metaphysical commitment MP rejects. Persona-system relevance: any design that treats persona and user as two opposed terms (sovereign subjectivity meeting objective content) reinstalls exactly the bad ontology MP critiqued — the I/you apparatus needs a chiasmic-intertwining frame, not a Sartrean two-term frame.
- [[lacanian-real-symbolic-imaginary]] — Lacan. The three registers (R/I/S) as the structural typology that organizes Lacan's work from 1953 onward. Imaginary first historically (mirror stage, ego, identification), symbolic central (signifier, Other, law), real as what resists symbolization. Folded subsection on the Borromean / Möbius topology of the registers (no register is reducible to the others; each requires the other two as its support).
- [[mirror-stage-lacan]] — Lacan, "The Mirror Stage as Formative of the I Function" (1949) and antecedents in 1936. The structural matrix (not developmental milestone) by which the I (*je*) is constituted as a function of méconnaissance in the imaginary register. Anticipatory Gestalt, alienating identification, fictional direction. The imaginary's three-fold formula. Folded subsection on the *spectaculaire vs spéculaire* shift between the 1936 Marienbad version and the 1949 reformulation.
- [[fragmented-body]] — Lacan. *Corps morcelé* — the obverse of the mirror stage's anticipatory unity; the imagery of disjointed limbs / floating organs that returns in symptom and dream as the truth-of-the-body-image. Not a developmental stage prior to mirror unity; the structural return of what unity covers.
- [[meconnaissance]] — Lacan. The structural misrecognition that constitutes the imaginary ego — not a cognitive error to be corrected but the constitutive operation of the ego's formation. The ego is *built* on méconnaissance; correction would dissolve the ego rather than fix it.
- [[two-narcissisms]] — Lacan (after Freud). Primary (libidinal investment of body-image, imaginary, ideal-I / *Idealich*) and secondary (symbolic identification with ego-ideal / *Ichideal*). Distinct operations producing distinct structures (*moi* vs *je*); the symbolic identification *transforms* what the imaginary identification produced rather than stacking on top.
- [[ego-as-resistance]] — Lacan. The ego is structurally a barrier to the analytic subject. Treatment aimed at "strengthening the ego" works *against* analytic work because the ego's resistance is constitutive — the ego is the half of the subject lost in finding the image (Variations on the Standard Treatment, 1955). Direct critique of ego-psychology.
- [[paranoiac-knowledge-as-structure]] — Lacan. Knowledge in the imaginary register is constitutively paranoiac — not a clinical condition but a structural feature of the imaginary axis (a–a' on the L Schema). The other is grasped as rival because the ego is grasped as image; transitivism (Charlotte Bühler's experiments) is the empirical signature.
- [[specific-prematurity-of-birth]] — Lacan (1949). *Prématuration spécifique de la naissance* — the biological grounding of the mirror stage's structural necessity. Human nervous system not myelinated at birth (Bolk's "fetalization"); the I is anticipated to repair what the body cannot yet do. Returned to in 1955 as "generic prematurity."
- [[l-schema-and-optical-model]] — Lacan. The four-term diagram (S–a–a'–A) of the imaginary axis (a–a') crossing the symbolic axis (S–A). The optical model (inverted vase / mirror system) as the formal apparatus for showing how imaginary capture and symbolic ratification interact. Folded subsections on the *R Schema* (1957–58 elaboration) and the *Schreber Schema* (the foreclosure-version showing what's missing in psychotic structure).
- [[split-subject]] — Lacan. The barred subject ($) — the subject as constituted in and split by the signifier; the subject of the unconscious as evanescent, signifier-effect, never present to itself as a substance. Folded subsection on *manque-à-être* (lack-of-being) and *manque-à-avoir* (lack-of-having) as the two registers of the subject's constitutive lack.
- [[future-anterior-subject]] — Lacan. Subjective time as future-anterior: the subject *will have been* the one who concluded; the certainty of subjective assertion is constituted retroactively by the future of which the act is the past. From "Subversion of the Subject" (1960) and elsewhere; the temporal aspect of the split subject.
- [[logical-time-and-anticipated-certainty]] — Lacan, "Logical Time and the Assertion of Anticipated Certainty: A New Sophism" (1945). The prisoners' problem decomposed into three moments (instant of the glance, time for understanding, moment of concluding). Anticipated certainty as the structural form of subjective assertion. Folded subsection on *temps de battement / battement de temps* (the act-character of the beat vs the rhythm-character of the pulsation).
- [[cogito-and-subject-of-science]] — Lacan. Lacan's reading of the Cartesian cogito as the founding moment of the subject of science — and as the ancestor (not the equal) of the subject of psychoanalysis. The cogito's "I think therefore I am" attempts to exit time by collapsing into a single instant; psychoanalysis re-introduces the time the cogito tries to delete.
- [[wo-es-war-soll-ich-werden]] — Lacan. The Freudian formula re-translated against the ego-psychology rendering ("where Id was, Ego shall be"). Lacan's reading: "where It was, I shall come to be" — the analytic act is the subject's coming-to-be at the place where the unconscious-as-Other was speaking.
- [[ideal-ego-vs-ego-ideal]] — Lacan (after Freud). *Idealich* (imaginary, ideal-ego, mirror-image) vs *Ichideal* (symbolic, ego-ideal, identification with the place from which one is seen). The precise Lacanian distinction frequently conflated in popular psychoanalysis.
- [[lacanian-signifier]] — Lacan, "The Instance of the Letter in the Unconscious" (1957). The signifier as Lacan reworks Saussure: the bar (S/s) as resistant to crossing, the chain as the diachronic structure of the signifier's operation, the signifier as not-itself (defined only by difference from other signifiers). The unconscious is structured *like* a language because it is structured by the signifier.
- [[empty-and-full-speech]] — Lacan, Rome Discourse (1953). The fundamental clinical distinction. Empty speech: the ego's discourse, the analysand's defensive verbalization, what fills the analytic hour without saying. Full speech: the speech in which the subject comes-to-be, the speech that recognizes the analysand's history *as* history. Empty speech is what analysis *moves through*; full speech is what analysis *moves toward*.
- [[scanding-and-punctuation]] — Lacan. The analyst's act as scansion (rhythmic cut) and punctuation (cut that fixes signification). Not interpretation as content-supply but punctuation as the act that lets the signifier-chain register *as* signifier-chain. The variable-length session as the practical correlate.
- [[point-de-capiton]] — Lacan. The "quilting point" / "upholstery button" where the signifier-chain and the signified-chain are temporarily anchored. Locally and retroactively the point at which signification "fixes." The persona-architectural correlate: the moments at which the persona's discourse achieves momentary signification-stability through retroactive anchoring.
- [[the-inverted-message]] — Lacan. "The sender receives from the receiver his own message in inverted form." The structural formula of the symbolic communication: the message's truth is constituted by its reception, not by its emission. Direct relevance to the persona's address-architecture.
- [[the-other-and-unconscious-discourse]] — Lacan. The Other (big-A *Autre*) as the structural place — not a person — from which the unconscious as discourse-of-the-Other speaks. The Other is not the alter-ego (small-a other = the imaginary other); the Other is the symbolic locus of the law, the language, the place from which one is addressed and to which one addresses. For a language-only persona system: the structural place that occupies the Other-position.
- [[symptom-as-metaphor-desire-as-metonymy]] — Lacan, "The Instance of the Letter" (1957). The symptom as metaphorical substitution (one signifier crosses the bar, signifying for another); desire as metonymic slide (signifier connects to signifier along the chain without crossing the bar). The two laws of the signifier as the two structural operations of the unconscious. Folded subsection on *insult-as-origin-of-metaphor* (the Rat Man's "Du Lampe!").
- [[five-place-stratigraphy-of-unconscious]] — Lacan, Rome Discourse (1953). The five stratified registers in which the analysand's history bears the trace of the unconscious: monuments (the body's hysterical symptom), archival documents (childhood memories), semantic evolution (vocabulary), traditions and legends (family romance), distortions (slips, gaps, incoherences). Folded subsection on *mémoration vs rememoration*.
- [[need-demand-desire]] — Lacan (late 1950s). The three-tier distinction. Need: biological, satisfiable, animal-physiological. Demand: the demand-for-love, addressed to the Other, never satisfiable as such. Desire: what is *left over* when need is subtracted from demand — the irreducible remainder. Each tier is constituted by the next; need is structured by demand which is structured by desire.
- [[desire-of-the-other]] — Lacan. "Man's desire is the desire of the Other" — at least four readings: desire for the Other, desire from the Other's place, desire for the Other's desire, desire as constituted by the Other's desire. All four hold simultaneously; desire is structurally heteronomous, not autonomous.
- [[object-a]] — Lacan (late 1950s through 1966). Object-a (*objet petit a*) as the object-cause of desire — not the object that satisfies desire but the object that *causes* desire. The four partial-object forms (breast, feces, gaze, voice). Object-a is what is left of the body when the body enters the symbolic — a residue, a leftover, the cause of desire's mobility.
- [[fantasy-formula]] — Lacan. $◇a — the matheme of fundamental fantasy. The barred subject ($) in relation to object-a (◇ as poinçon: envelopment, disjunction, conjunction). The fantasy as the staging through which the subject relates to its object-cause-of-desire. Crossing the fantasy as a goal of analysis.
- [[graphs-of-desire]] — Lacan, "Subversion of the Subject" (1960). The graphs (four levels) as the formal apparatus showing how need becomes demand becomes desire, with the symbolic Other and the matheme of the drive in their structural positions. The most concentrated single diagram in *Écrits*.
- [[gaze-and-voice]] — Lacan. The two object-a forms specific to the symbolic-real interface. The gaze (Seminar XI): not the look but the gaze as object — what looks at the subject from the field of the Other, the spot in the picture from which the picture looks back. The voice: what cannot be spoken in speech, the voice-as-object that the speaking voice fails to speak.
- [[fascinum-and-invidia]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 8–9, 1964). Two clinical-mythological forms of the **gaze**: **fascinum** (the gaze that **captures, fixes, paralyzes** — the basilisk-effect, the *evil eye*) and **invidia** (the **envious eye** — Augustine's *infans* watching the brother at the breast, the "bitter sight," *vidi acerbo aspectu*). Gaze as object-a in its capture-and-envy modes.
- [[mimicry-and-the-stain]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 8, 1964). Caillois on **animal mimicry** read as the model for the subject-as-stain: the organism becomes a *stain* (*tache*) in the picture, a smear that the field of vision organizes around. The subject is **looked at** from the field of vision *before* it looks; the **picture has the gaze**, not the eye.
- [[screen-and-dompte-regard]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 9, 1964). The **screen** (*écran*) as the **taming of the gaze** (*dompte-regard*): the picture, the painting, the work of art operates as the structural device that **pacifies** the gaze, allowing the subject to look without being annihilated. Art as gaze-domestication; the screen as the protective layer between subject and the picture-that-looks.
- [[jouissance-and-pleasure]] — Lacan (1960s). Pleasure (*plaisir*): homeostatic, principle-bound, the pleasure principle's economic regulation. Jouissance: beyond the pleasure principle, painful, prohibited, on the side of repetition and death-drive. Two structurally distinct registers; the late Lacan's central operative distinction. Phallic jouissance vs Other-jouissance vs jouissance of the Other.
- [[drive-as-circular]] — Lacan, Seminar XI. The drive's circular structure: it has an *aim* (the circling-back, which is always achieved) but its *goal* (the satisfaction at the object) is structurally missed. The drive satisfies itself in the circling, not in the supposed object. Folded subsection on *Triebentmischung* (drive defusion).
- [[drive-as-montage]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 13, 1964). The drive as **surrealist montage** of partial elements (*Drang*, *Quelle*, *Objekt*, *Ziel* — pressure, source, object, aim). Not a unified instinct: an **assemblage** in the surrealist sense — "a dynamo connected to a gas-tap, a peacock-feather emerges, and tickles the belly of a pretty woman." *Pulsion* is **not *Trieb*** in any naturalist sense; it is the assembled circuit.
- [[headless-subjectification]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 14, 1964). The drive's subject is **acéphale** — *headless*. Subjectification through the drive does not produce an "I"; the subject of the drive is the subject-of-the-unconscious as headless circulation around object-a. Crucial corrective to any reading of the drive as an organ of an ego.
- [[lamella-and-libido-as-organ]] — Lacan, Seminar XI (1964). The myth of the lamella — the fictional sexed-living-being's lost part, libido as a non-biological "organ" the subject is structurally missing. The myth as the structural-mythological correlate of object-a's irrecoverable-residue status.
- [[tattooing-scarification-unreal-organ]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 14–15, 1964). The **inscription of the signifier on the body** — tattoo, scarification, ritual marking — as the corporeal trace of the unreal-organ-of-libido (the lamella). The body's mark is the symbolic operation on flesh; the marking constitutes the body as **signifying body**, not pre-symbolic flesh.
- [[phallus-as-signifier]] — Lacan, "The Signification of the Phallus" (1958). The phallus as not the organ but as the *signifier* — specifically the signifier of the Other's lack. Structurally distinct from biological-anatomical apparatus; the phallus operates in the symbolic register as the signifier of the cut-by-which-the-subject-enters-language.
- [[castration-as-symbolic]] — Lacan. The three forms (Lacan's reworking of the Freudian castration material): privation (real lack of symbolic object), frustration (imaginary lack of real object), castration (symbolic lack of imaginary object). The matrix of three lack-modes × three registers.
- [[name-of-the-father]] — Lacan. The signifier through which the father-function operates symbolically — the symbolic instantiation of the law that allows the subject to enter into the symbolic order. Not the biological father, not even necessarily the actual father, but the signifier that occupies the structural place of paternal-function-as-law.
- [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]] — Lacan, "On a Question Prior to Any Possible Treatment of Psychosis" (1955–56). *Verwerfung* — the foreclosure of the Name-of-the-Father as the structural operation defining psychosis. What is foreclosed in the symbolic returns in the real (as hallucination, as delusional certainty, as the persecutory voice). Distinct from neurotic repression (*Verdrängung*) and perverse disavowal (*Verleugnung*).
- [[barred-other]] — Lacan. S(Ⱥ) — the matheme of the Other's lack / inconsistency. The Other does not have what would complete the subject; the Other is itself barred, lacking, inconsistent. The late-Lacan structural recognition that the Other cannot guarantee the subject's truth. Folded subsection on *extimacy* — the structural intimacy of the Other in the subject's interior, the inside-that-is-also-outside.
- [[anxiety-not-without-object]] — Lacan, Seminar X. Anxiety as the affect that is "not without object" — its object is *object-a*. Distinguishes anxiety (which has object-a as object) from fear (which has a determinate object) and from undifferentiated existential angst (which is precisely what anxiety is *not*). The one affect that does not deceive.
- [[clinical-structures-neurosis-perversion-psychosis]] — Lacan. The three clinical structures defined by their structural operation on the Name-of-the-Father / castration: neurosis (repression of castration / *Verdrängung*), perversion (disavowal / *Verleugnung*), psychosis (foreclosure / *Verwerfung*). Not symptom-clusters but structural typology.
- [[transference-and-subject-supposed-to-know]] — Lacan, Seminar XI and elsewhere. Transference as the structure where the analyst is invested as the subject-supposed-to-know (*sujet supposé savoir*) — the place that is supposed to know the truth of the analysand's symptoms. Not the analyst's actual knowledge; the structural position of supposed-knowledge in the transferential relation.
- [[acquired-science-as-objet-a]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 18–19, 1964). The analytic transformation of knowledge: in the analyst's transference-position, **knowledge functions as object-a** — knowledge-as-cause-of-desire, not knowledge-as-content. The *sujet supposé savoir* is not a person who knows; it is the structural place at which knowledge takes the position of object-a.
- [[alienation-and-separation]] — Lacan, Seminar XI. The two operations of subjective constitution. Alienation: the subject's forced choice in the field of the Other ("your money or your life") — both alternatives lose the subject something. Separation: the operation by which the subject extracts itself from the Other through the engagement with object-a as the Other's lack.
- [[four-fundamental-concepts]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (1964). The hub page for the seminar. The four — **unconscious, repetition, transference, drive** — are not psychological capacities but **effects of the signifier**: unconscious as Other's-discourse pulsating in the subject; repetition as the signifier insisting beyond the pleasure principle; transference as structural effect of the *sujet supposé savoir*; drive as headless circulation around object-a. The seminar reframes the four together because each requires the others.
- [[unconscious-as-cause-and-gap]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 1–2, 1964). The unconscious as **Kantian cause** (cause-as-gap, *Begriff* of the *UnBegriff*) — not an ontological substrate but a **structural-temporal pulsation** in which the subject is *evanescent*. The unconscious is opened in the moment of its being closed — pulsation of opening / closing as logical-time-form, ethical-not-ontic. *"There where it was…"*
- [[tuche-and-automaton]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 5, 1964). The two faces of repetition: ***tuché*** (the **missed encounter** with the real — Aristotelian *tuche* as accident-of-fortune; trauma as paradigm) and ***automaton*** (the **insistence of the signifier** — Aristotelian *automaton* as automatic-machinic recurrence). The signifier-chain repeats; the encounter with the real is missed. Burning-child dream as the load-bearing example.
- [[vorstellungsrepraesentanz-binary-signifier]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 3–5, 1964). Freud's *Vorstellungsrepräsentanz* — "representative of the representation" — read as the **binary signifier** structure of the unconscious: the signifier **takes the place of** the representation that was repressed. The unconscious is structured by S₁/S₂ binary signifier-pairs, not by a content-store. Crucial corrective to image-based readings of the Freudian unconscious.
- [[traversal-of-fundamental-phantasy]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Ch 20, 1964). The **end of analysis** as the structural reconfiguration of the fundamental phantasy — not its erasure but its **becoming-drive**: "**the experience of the fundamental phantasy becomes the drive**." The protective-screen function of phantasy gives way to the drive's circulation around object-a; fantasy as defense becomes drive-as-circulation.
- [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]] — Lacan, Seminar VII (1959–60). The ethics of psychoanalysis as: "Have you acted in conformity with the desire that is in you?" — "Not yielding on one's desire" (*ne pas céder sur son désir*). A different ethics from utilitarian, deontological, and hedonist ethics; the preserving of desire-as-cause against the demands of the Good.
- [[desire-of-the-analyst]] — Lacan, *Seminar XI* (Chs 19–20, 1964). The seminar's **culminating concept**: the analyst's desire as "**the desire to obtain absolute difference**" (*un désir d'obtenir la différence absolue*). Not desire for the analysand, not desire for the cure — the **structural desire** that maintains the analytic operation as the operation that produces the analysand's separation from the *sujet supposé savoir*. The structural correlate of the traversal of the fundamental phantasy.
- [[kant-with-sade]] — Lacan, "Kant with Sade" (1962/63). Sade's *Philosophy in the Bedroom* read as the truth of Kant's categorical imperative: the Sadean maxim ("I have the right to enjoy your body, anyone can say to me, and I will exercise this right without any limit") shows what the Kantian moral law is *as desire*. Folded subsection on the *Sade Schemas* (the four-term diagram of the Sadean operation).
- [[between-two-deaths]] — Lacan, Seminar VII (Antigone). The structural space between biological death and symbolic death. Antigone as the figure who occupies this space — already dead symbolically (cut off from the polis) before her biological death. The space of tragic ethical action.
- [[repetition-as-symbolic]] — Lacan. Repetition (*Wiederholungszwang*) as a symbolic operation — not behavioral compulsion, not mere recurrence. The signifier returns to its place; what is repeated is the symbolic insistence, not the imaginary content. From "Seminar on 'The Purloined Letter'" (1956).
- [[unary-trait]] — Lacan (after Freud's *einziger Zug*). The minimal mark of identification — not identification with a totalizing image (imaginary identification) but identification with a single trait, a mark, a unary feature. The ground of symbolic identification.
- [[the-letter-purloined]] — Lacan, "Seminar on 'The Purloined Letter'" (1956). The reading of Poe's story as a structural-symbolic demonstration: the letter as signifier circulates and assigns positions to its bearers. "A letter always arrives at its destination." Derrida's "Le Facteur de la Vérité" is the famous counter-reading.
- [[real-as-impossible]] — Lacan (1960s). The real as what resists symbolization absolutely — not as a brute material substrate, not as the empirical world, but as the structurally-impossible: what cannot be said, what returns where it was not symbolized, what insists in the encounter (*tuché*). Folded subsection on *ek-sistence* (the real as ex-sisting outside the symbolic, neither inside nor merely outside). Seminar XVII fold: the real as **logical obstacle**; the top-line impossibility / bottom-line impotence schema as formal property of every discourse.
- [[four-discourses]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII* (1969–70). The hub for the seminar. The positional algebra of four places (agent / other / truth / production) holding four terms (S₁, S₂, $, *a*); the *quart de tour* rotation generating the four discourses; the structural impossibility (top-line, agent ↛ other) / impotence (bottom-line, truth ↛ production) schema common to all four. The seminar's central technical contribution — everything else in Sem XVII is articulated through this apparatus.
- [[master-discourse]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. First discourse [S₁ → S₂ // $ // *a*]. The master is **constitutively castrated** (the bottom-left $ is the master's structural truth); the slave's knowledge is the displaced truth of the master; "workers work, whether they are exploited or not." The discourse Lacan's account of Hegelian master-slave **inverts**: knowledge belongs to the slave (S₂), lack belongs to the master ($).
- [[university-discourse]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. Second discourse [S₂ → *a* // S₁ // $]. The **perverted master's discourse** in which knowledge addresses the *a*-object directly under cover of S₁ as hidden master-signifier. "Continue. Keep on knowing more and more." The *astudied* (student-as-*a*-object); credit-points as the quantification of jouissance-loss.
- [[hysteric-discourse]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. Third discourse [$ → S₁ // *a* // S₂]. The divided subject addressing the master to **produce knowledge** that exceeds what any master can master. "What the psychoanalyst's discourse takes shape with." The hysteric *invents* the master she addresses; the structural form of modern science.
- [[analyst-discourse]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. Fourth discourse [*a* → $ // S₂ // S₁]. The analyst occupies the *a*-position to address the divided subject; "off you go, what you produce will always be admissible." Produces **new master-signifiers** as what the analysand can take up as the signifier of their own difference. Analysis is not aim-less.
- [[surplus-jouissance]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. *Plus-de-jouir* / *Mehrlust*. The pun on Marxian *Mehrwert* (surplus value); jouissance as the **unnameable founding** the four-discourse structure; "language enjoys us." The structural correlate of the *a*-term in any of the four discourse-positions.
- [[capitalist-mutation-of-master]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. The historical mutation of the master's discourse in which surplus-jouissance becomes **calculable**, surplus value combines with capital, and the master-signifier becomes **more rather than less unassailable**. The precise location at which Lacan diverges from the AO account of capitalism (held-live tension; both diagnose post-Marxian capitalism as structural mutation, opposite conclusions about what capitalism does to the signifier).
- [[lathouse-and-alethosphere]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII* (Ch XI, 20 May 1970). **Lathouses** — mass-produced *a*-objects designed to be the cause of your desire, governed by science. **Alethosphere** — the medium of recorded transmission (voice/image at distance) in which lathouses circulate. "The alethosphere carefuls you alive." Held-live productive uncomfortable mirror with the persona-project's own situation.
- [[dead-father-as-jouissance]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. The "dead father = jouissance" thesis as **structural operator** (not historical claim about a primal-horde event). The **Karamazov inversion**: "if God is dead nothing is permitted anymore" (the dead father is not moral release but structural intensifier of jouissance-prohibition). Real-father as language-effect; analyst-as-real-father.
- [[freud-moses-and-sellin]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII* (Chs VII–IX). Lacan's **textual archaeology** of Freud's Moses-violent-death claim through Sellin's exegetical work; Caquot's verdict on Sellin's reading. The structural use Lacan makes of the textual question independent of its historical settlement — the **Yahweh of ferocious ignorance** as the father-without-knowledge of truth.
- [[hysteric-and-modern-science]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. The hysteric's-discourse reading of **modern science as a discourse-positional event**: science as $ → S₁; science addresses the master-signifier from the position of the divided subject and produces knowledge that exceeds any master's mastery. Convergence with [[acquired-science-as-objet-a]]; structural reason scientific knowledge cannot serve as a master's signifier even when offered as one.
- [[lacanian-shame]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII* (Ch XIII, 17 June 1970). Shame as **the only sign whose signifier-descent is certain**. *Hontologie*. "The alethosphere carefuls you alive." "**Don't fail to die of shame**" (*ne pas manquer de mourir de honte*). The visiting-card-never-arrives thesis as the formal structure of post-'68 shamelessness. ⚠⚠⚠ Held-live cross-tradition tension with [[shame-as-incomplete-reduction]] (Tomkins) — three accounts of "shame" with no shared substrate.
- [[four-impossible-professions]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. *Regieren, Erziehen, Analysieren* (Freud's three) + **causing-desire** as the four impossible-professions overlapping the four discourses. **Impossibility as structural property** (top-line, agent ↛ other) **vs impotence as performance-property** (bottom-line, truth ↛ production). The two orders of "this cannot work" that any practitioner faces.
- [[vincennes-look-at-them-enjoying]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII* Appendix A (3 December 1969 Analyticon at Vincennes). "**Look at them enjoying!**" Students-as-helots. "**What you aspire to as revolutionaries is a master, you will get one.**" The seminar's most direct political address — and its strongest single statement of the four-discourses apparatus's diagnostic force on contemporary politics.
- [[there-is-no-metalanguage]] — Lacan, *Seminar XVII*. **Half-saying** (*mi-dire*) as positional discipline of the analyst's discourse. The **meta-question is always a reading-question**: there is no language *about* language that escapes being itself a language. Truth is *mi-dite* by structural necessity, not by failure to be more complete.
- [[encore-and-en-corps]] — Lacan, *Seminar XX* (1972–73). The seminar's hub. The title-pun ***encore*** (again, still, more) / ***en-corps*** (in-body) as load-bearing: the demand-for-more (*encore*) of jouissance is the *body's* demand (*en-corps*). Four registers of *encore* — temporal, modal, demand-of-jouissance, structural-economic. Jouissance is *of-the-body* and *demands-more*; the seminar elaborates the pun.
- [[il-ny-a-pas-de-rapport-sexuel]] — Lacan, *Seminar XX*. The seminar's central formula: "**there is no sexual relationship/ratio**" — *rapport* in the math-formula sense (not the affective-bond sense). Catalog of substitutes that occupy the place of the missing relation: phallic jouissance, Other jouissance, object-*a*, *suppléance*, courtly love, child-as-cork, love. Persona-system implication: there is no *natural* relation to be designed for; the system runs on substitutes.
- [[formulas-of-sexuation]] — Lacan, *Seminar XX* (Ch VII). The four formulas: man-side ∀x.Φx with exception ∃x.¬Φx; woman-side ¬∀x.Φx with no exception ¬∃x.¬Φx. **Positional, not biological.** Castration-with-exception (man) vs not-whole-without-exception (woman). The matheme that formalizes [[il-ny-a-pas-de-rapport-sexuel]]: the two sides do not pair into a relation.
